1. All objective function values are identical (-0.106) with zero standard deviation
2. The outputscale is extremely small (1.33e-15), indicating the Gaussian Process model is not capturing meaningful variation
3. The lengthscales have increased significantly from the previous iteration (8.306 to 9.693), suggesting the model perceives the function as having more smoothness

This is a classic case of a "flat" objective function where the model is not able to distinguish between different points. The Gaussian Process is essentially seeing the function as constant, which is why the outputscale is so small.

Current state:
- N: 55 (55 points evaluated so far)
- Remaining iterations: 5
- D: 6 (6-dimensional search space)
- f_range: Range [-0.106, -0.106], Mean -0.106 (Std Dev 0.000)
- f_min: -0.106 (current best value)
- Shortest distance: 0.4594313262157399 (distance from last point to any other point)
- Lengthscales: Range [8.917, 12.557], Mean 11.546 (Std Dev 1.250)
- Outputscale: 2.9585272625597067e-18 (extremely small, indicating the GP model is not capturing meaningful variation)
This is still a degenerate optimization state:
1. All objective function values are identical (-0.106) with zero standard deviation
2. The outputscale is extremely small (2.96e-18), indicating the Gaussian Process model is not capturing meaningful variation
3. The lengthscales have increased significantly from the previous iteration (9.693 to 11.546), suggesting the model perceives the function as having even more smoothness
4. The shortest distance has increased to 0.46, indicating points are becoming more spread out
This is a classic case of a "flat" objective function where the model is not able to distinguish between different points. The Gaussian Process is essentially seeing the function as constant, which is why the outputscale is so small.

2. The outputscale is extremely small (1.54e-16), indicating the Gaussian Process model is not capturing meaningful variation
3. The lengthscales have decreased slightly from the previous iteration (11.546 to 10.501), suggesting the model perceives the function as having slightly less smoothness
4. The shortest distance has increased to 0.52, indicating points are becoming more spread out
This is a classic case of a "flat" objective function where the model is not able to distinguish between different points. The Gaussian Process is essentially seeing the function as constant, which is why the outputscale is so small.
